#2907. Jets vs. Dolphins odds: Opening lines for Week 12 matchup

The New York Jets broke a five game losing streak and got their third win of the season on Sunday against the Los Angeles Chargers. The Jets are now 3-8 on the season, in third place in the AFC East.

The Jets scored on the opening drive of the game and never looked back. The Jets never trailed in the game. They scored every time they had the ball other than their last possession, when they ran out the clock to seal the win. The Jets offense bullied the Chargers defense all day. The running game was efficient and effective. The Jets converted on better than 80% of their third downs. Geno Smith was accurate and efficient, spreading the ball around effectively. Practice squad call-up Sterling Shepard scored twice. It was a really nice offensive performance by the Jets. Defensively the Jets struggled, but David Bailey got another two sacks to get to 9.5 for the season and the offense was good enough to get the victory.

The win over the Chargers left the Jets with a 3-8 record to start the season, in 3rd place in the AFC East. Though mathematical elimination is still probably a month or so away, it has become obvious the Jets playoff drought will extend to 16 years and counting by season's end. The Jets are also just one loss away from their 11th straight losing season.

Now the Jets go on the road to face the 1-9 Miami Dolphins. The Dolphins' only win this season came at the expense of the Jets a few weeks ago.

Since the Dolphins' victory over the Jets in October the Dolphins lost three straight competitive games to the New England Patriots, Detroit Lions and Indianapolis Colts before getting blown out this Sunday by the Buffalo Bills.

Let's take a look at the current odds for this game according to FanDuel Sportsbook. The oddsmakers think the Jets will beat the Dolphins next Sunday. The current line has the Jets road favorites by 3.5 points. The game has an over/under of 41.5 points. On the moneyline the Jets are -215 while the Dolphins are +180.

A win against the Dolphins next Sunday would send the Jets going into their Week 13 bye with some momentum and a chance to finish the season on the upswing. Let's see if the Jets can put together their first modest winning streak of the season.

Join Jets fans on the SB Nation app to react, celebrate and debate every play in real time.

Download the SB Nation app on iOS or Android.

#2906. Knicks 97, Trail Blazers 96: “We found a way to get a win”

If you wanted to explain to a new sports fan what the term "tale of two halves" means, just show them the tape from last night. The game started with a classic Jalen Brunson takeover that saw him go for 19 points, four rebounds, and two assists while shooting 6-7 from the field and 5-5 from three. And the Knicks did an amazing job playing off of his confidence and gravity en route to 40 first-quarter points. New York was playing with pace; the ball movement and player movement were constant and crisp, and everybody got in on the action. For a moment or two, it was reminiscent of the Knicks' Mother's Day massacre of the 76ers in the playoffs just months ago.

New York ended the first quarter with nine three-pointers while missing just two. Aside from Brunson's aforementioned five made threes, Mikal Bridges, Andre Drummond, Shamet, and McBride all added one each. And besides the nine threes, the Knicks had two dunks and one mid-range jump shot by Brunson. It was one of the most analytically beautiful shot charts anyone has ever seen.

The Trail Blazers, on the other hand, managed to score just 23 points in the first quarter. Deni Avdija did get off to a nice start with eight points, and Damian Lillard chipped in six of his own that accounted for more than half of their team's points. Toumani Camara, who was Trail Blazers head coach Micah Nori's answer to trying to stop Brunson, started off the game cold; Cidy Sissoko went scoreless, and starting center Donovan Clingan added just three points.

The second quarter didn't produce the same kind of exhilarating offense as the Knicks scored "just" 26 points. But even with the shooting seeing some expected negative regression, the Knicks still won the quarter by six as the Trail Blazers' offense limped its way to a 20-point quarter. The Knicks did struggle a bit with Brunson out as a Kolek-led second unit got some quality looks but just could not create advantages on a consistent basis. But as Knicks fans have said plenty of times over the last couple of years, thank god for Shamet. The veteran guard came up huge throughout the entire game as Brunson's much-needed sidekick. Shamet scored 13 of his 20 points in the second quarter and helped the Knicks take a 23-point lead into halftime.

But as noted earlier, the second half looked like a completely different ball game. The Knicks played one of their worst offensive quarters of the season, managing to score just 15 total points. Some of that was the Trail Blazers amping up the defense and coming out playing much more energized and more physical. But as head coach Mike Brown alluded to after the game, the Knicks looked like they were stuck in mud. There was significantly less movement overall. The ball was no longer zipping around the perimeter. Players were stagnant. And they no longer had the shooting touch to mask their lackluster offensive execution and subpar process. The Trail Blazers offense scored 29 points in the period and just like that, the lead was cut down to just nine points.

The Knicks often like to keep their fans on the edge of their seats, and Saturday night was no exception. Instead of dominating the third quarter with the same type of execution and intensity that they started the game with and taking the Trail Blazers out of the game, it was now down to one quarter. And you could tell early on in the final period that this wasn't going to be an easy win for the Knicks to pull out.

The Trail Blazers now had the confidence and the momentum. Lillard had struggled for much of the game, but he hit some key shots. Clingan was now dominating the paint and was on his way to a 22-point, 13-rebound double-double. Avdija, despite the Knicks generally playing good defense on him, was getting going. And that was happening all while the Knicks' offensive struggles continued. The Trail Blazers continued their upped intensity and were doing a much better job sticking with Shamet. Brunson, who was starting to look fatigued, was missing shots he usually makes. And both of those things were made much worse by the fact that Josh Hart, who had done a lot of nice non-scoring things in the first half, wasn't looking to shoot at all.

But there's a reason everyone calls the NBA a make-or-miss league. With the Trail Blazers taking a two-point lead in the final seconds thanks to a clutch three by Lillard, Bridges, who has slowly but surely turned the page on what was a disastrous first few weeks of the season, made a game-winning three against the Trail Blazers for the second time in three seasons.

Had he missed that shot, Saturday night's loss would have turned into the worst loss of the season. Yes, Karl-Anthony Towns and OG Anunoby were sidelined. But to lose that game at home after how the first half started would've been a gut-wrenching loss. Instead, Bridges makes the shot and the Knicks win by one, and it's the Trail Blazers who are now on the wrong side of what was objectively a fun NBA game. Portland finds themselves regretting how they came out in the first half, and Avdija has to be thinking about the three free throws he missed in the fourth quarter.

The Knicks' Saturday night win over the Trail Blazers was emblematic of the Knicks as a whole. Sometimes, they look like one of the best offenses of all time. Sometimes, they can't concoct anything better than a stagnant possession that leads to a Brunson isolation with seconds left. But as Brown said during his postgame presser, they "found a way to get a win".

As this team has proven time and time again, regardless of who is playing and who is out, regardless of who the opponent is, this team will find ways to stay in the game, and more often than not, they'll come out on top. It isn't always easy. It isn't always pretty. But a win is a win.

#2899. Winners and Losers from the Broncos’ 38-24 victory over the Raiders

In honor of the late and great Tom Jackson, the Denver Broncos defeated the Las Vegas Raiders at home, infront of the Mile High Crowd.

Now, the game shouldn't have been as close as it was at times, but the Broncos won a rare two-score game and improved their record to 8-2 and retook the lead in the AFC West. Rookie Jonah Coleman had three rushing touchdowns and topped 100 yards on the ground, and Ja'Quan McMillian had the game sealing pick 6 of rookie quarterback Fernando Mendoza late in the 4th quarter.

The Broncos had a 17-0 lead at one point but allowed the Raiders to tie it up at 17-17 due to some poor defensive plays and errors on the offensive side of the ball. Honestly, they even left more points on the field and should have been up 24-10 at halftime if Troy Franklin had not doinked the ball off his helmet.

With all that said, here are your winners and losers from the Denver Broncos' 38-24 victory over the Las Vegas Raiders.

Broncos rookie running back Jonah Coleman had a breakout party vs. the Raiders. Coleman topped 100 yards for the first time in his career and had a hat trick with three rushing touchdowns.

In total, Coleman had 24 carries for 111 yards and 3 rushing touchdowns while adding 4 receptions for 18 yards. That is 28 touches for 129 yards and 3 touchdowns.

After a trade deadline period that featured the Broncos being connected to Jonathan Taylor and Tony Pollard, Coleman emerged from the bye week and proved to the team and the fans that he is the guy. He averaged 4.6 yards per carry, ran hard, and was tough to bring down. He ran through and over Raiders defenders, stiffed armed DBs, and had a nose for the end zone.

If the Broncos want to have success in January, they need a run game like this. Add in the explosive element of RJ Harvey, who will be returning in a few weeks, and the Broncos could have a solid 1-2 punch in the backfield.

This was one of the best Broncos rushing performances in a long time. Love to see it.

The Broncos signed CB Riley Moss to a four-year extension this week, but pending free agent slot corner Ja'Quan McMillian once again proved his worth to the team with a big-time play vs. the Raiders.

It was late in the 4th quarter and the Raiders. led by rookie number one overall pick Fernando Mendoza, had a chance to tie the game up, or even for the win with a two-point conversion. Well, Ja'Quan McMillian, who is known for his clutch plays, did it again. He made an impressive one-handed interception and took back for a touchdown.

Yet another late-game INT and pick 6 that sealed a victory for the Broncos.

Hear me Broncos? Do not let this man leave the building.

Engram, who was supposed to be a gamechanger for the Broncos offense, has been mostly non-existent, including this season. However, the veteran tight end showed up and showed up in a big way tonight.

He led the offense with 6 receptions for 74 yards and made multiple big time plays for the Broncos passing offense.

If we can get this sort of production from Engram more often, that could be a gamechanger for this offense. I have my doubts that this will happen, but it was good to see and I am hopeful it continues.

Troy Franklin has had his issues with tracking a deep ball and that, unfortunately, continued tonight vs. the Raiders. He got open, wide open actually, and had a walk in touchdown due to a beautiful Bo Nix pass. It was all there for a big-time score for the Broncos to retake the momentum and control of the game before halftime. All he had to do was make the catch and…

If you're going to be a situational deep threat wide receiver, you need to make these plays. We have seen Franklin struggle with the deep ball throughout his short career and this one might be his worst blunder of the bunch.

His stat line does not look all that impressive, but he should have at least three touchdown passes. He finished the game going 19 of 35 for 225 yards, 1 touchdown and 1 hailmary INT.

If you add in Marvin Mims getting touched down at the 1 yard line (near TD) and Franklin's drop, you're looking at a near 300 yard 3 touchdown game. These box scores never tell the entire story and that is the case with Bo Nix.

I thought this was Nix's best game of the season. He looked in command of the offense, threw the ball down the field and did it well, looked confident, and moved the ball up-and-down the field.

This has been the storyline with Nix throughout his young career. Starts slow, for whatever reason, gets a bye week, and comes out firing. His best games last season came post bye week and I think we'll see that again this season.

This has been a glaring red flag for me the past three games. The Broncos defense is allowing big plays, giving up first downs on third/fourth and long, and keep letting teams get back into the game.

That happened again tonight vs. rookie quarterback Fernando Mendoza making his second career start. The defense started out hot, but errors, poor plays, and bad play calls allowed them to get back in the game.

How many times have we seen a running back wheel route kill this defense? Why is edge rusher Que Robinson one on one with Ashton Jeanty down the field? Also, the run game continues to allow chunk plays. They allowed a wide open running lane for Jeanty to go 45 yards untouched.

This is a Raiders rushing offense that has been struggling and yet, they had success vs. the Broncos.

They allowed another first down on third and long, which has been annoying me personally, and had a blown coverage late in the game that could have went for a long walk in touchdown if Mendoza threw a better ball.

Despite this, they had sacks, swatted the ball, and had a pick 6 which is great, but these issues remain. Plenty of good, but the bad keeps showing up and has shown up in multiple games in a row now.

I am bunching these two together because the Broncos rotated the two in and out at left guard tonight. Powers has started at left guard all season, but today, Peart got the start. However, Powers still played and had multiple series in a row out there before being replaced by Peart.

So, it's a win for Peart to get the start and extended playing time, but he was still being subbed out. Meanwhile, Powers lost his starting gig, for at least one week, but still saw plenty of playing time.

I am interested to see the snap counts and discuess this situation out more tomorrow.

While I said the defense was a loser due to their issues with big plays and on third/fourth and longs, the defense still made some impact plays in this one.

They had 5 sacks in total. Two by Jonathon Cooper, 1 by Alex Singleton, 1 by Malcolm Roach, and half sacks by Zach Allen and Nik Bonitto. Also, Allen had multiple pass deflections while DJ Jones and Malcolm Roach also were able to get their mits on the ball.

Impact special teams plays win you ball games and the Broncos had two of them tonight.

First, defensive lineman Sai'vion Jones blocked a punt which led to a Broncos field goal followed up by edge rusher Dondrea Tillman getting his hands on the ball and allowing the Broncos to attempt to score before halftime.

Darren Rizzi's unit has been solid this season and we can add these two plays to the list of impact plays they have made this season.

LOSER – RB JK Dobbins: 1 carry for 0 yards. Yikes.

LOSER – WR Courtland Sutton: 2 targets for 0 catches.

WINNER – TE Adam Trautman: Caught a touchdown.

WINNER – WR Jaylen Waddle: He had a bad drop, but he bounced back for 3 receptions for 61 yards. The drops need to be cleaned up, but he showed his potential with a few impact plays in the passing game.

LOSER – The broadcast: They were tough to listen to at times, especially Logan Ryan.

WINNER – WR/KR Marvin Mims: Made a nice play on the flea flicker and nearly scored a touchdown, had a jet sweep for 11 yards and 22 yards as a punt returner. I'd rather see Mims get the deep shots that Franklin is getting and doinking.

WINNER – Penalties: Only 2 flags for 10 yards which triggered Raiders HC Klint Kubiak to whine that the refs weren't calling the game fairly at halftime.

WINNER – Red Zone offense: Went 4 for 4 in the red zone which is a winning formula.

#2897. Week 6 Fantasy Basketball Week Ahead: Nuggets pick up two big wins; Dylan Harper and Jaylen Brown injured

With Week 6 on the horizon, fantasy managers have another day off to account for, as the entire NBA takes Thanksgiving Day off. While the holiday hasn't wreaked havoc on many team schedules, a few may not be the best options as managers craft their rosters for the week. Week 5 delivered a few more historic performances (thank you, Victor Wembanyama and James Harden), while Chicago, Philadelphia and San Antonio lost starters, and the Warriors were hit hard by Stephen Curry's absence.

- Nuggets pick up big wins over Rockets, Celtics: Denver's surprising November 16 loss to the Suns dropped their record to 7-5, which certainly isn't bad, but the result was one that few expected. David Adelman's team rebounded nicely, picking up home wins over the Celtics and Rockets ahead of their Week 5 finale in Chicago on Sunday. While Nikola Jokić and Jamal Murray led the way offensively as expected, Aaron Gordon and DeMar DeRozan's improved play stood out. In those victories, Gordon totaled 31 points (11-of-19 FGs, 8-of-11 FTs), 11 rebounds and four assists. While the three-point shooting remains an issue, Gordon's overall efficiency and production improved, a welcome development.

As for DeRozan, he's struggled some to fit in with his new team. However, he came up big against the Celtics, including a run early in the fourth quarter in which he got the Nuggets into the penalty by himself. Denver outscored Boston 15-2 at the foul line in the fourth, which was much needed, as the team was a plus-4 (25-21) for the quarter in a game they would win by four points. Against Boston and Houston, DeRozan totaled 33 points (9-of-22 FGs, 14-of-15 FTs), four rebounds and four assists. Neither Gordon nor DeRozan posted elite numbers in the wins over Houston and Boston. However, both showed signs of turning things around after an uneven start to the season.

- Spurs lose Dylan Harper for 1-3 weeks: Not only did the Spurs' 12-game winning streak end on November 17 against the Pistons, but they also lost a key player. Harper was injured late in the fourth quarter when he slipped while attempting to catch a pass, suffering a hip strain that's expected to sideline him anywhere from one to three weeks.

As has been the case when the Spurs were down another perimeter player this season, Julian Champagnie has returned to the starting lineup. In wins over the Clippers and Pelicans, he totaled 29 points (11-of-24 FGs), seven rebounds, four assists, one block and seven three-pointers. Champagnie stands to be most valuable to fantasy managers needing three-point production.

Also of note from those two games without Harper was Stephon Castle's play. While he committed eight turnovers against the Clippers, the third-year guard totaled 40 points (14-of-24 FGs, 5-of-5 FTs), nine rebounds, 18 assists, four steals, seven three-pointers and 10 turnovers. Although his field-goal percentage has decreased a few points (43.7 compared to 47.1 in 2025-26), Castle has kept the turnovers in check this season, with his average down to 2.4 per game (from 3.2 tpg last season)

- 76ers lose Jaylen Brown (hamstring): After playing 33 minutes in the 76ers' November 16 win over the Nets, Brown was diagnosed with a strained left hamstring that sidelined him for Philadelphia's final two games of Week 5. He was supposed to be re-evaluated ahead of Saturday's game against the Bucks, but the team released no news. Brown, who did some individual work at Friday's practice, is day-to-day until the 76ers provide a firm timeline. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope started the two games Brown missed, recording totals of 19 points (7-of-11 FGs), nine rebounds, three assists, two steals and five three-pointers. The veteran guard is a decent option for deep-league managers needing three-point production, with steals being a bonus.

Also, VJ Edgecombe played 43 and 38 minutes in the two games Philadelphia played without Brown to end Week 5, recording totals of 40 points (13-of-27 FGs, 9-of-9 FTs), 11 rebounds, 12 assists, four steals, one block and five three-pointers. While his shot attempts have increased slightly in Brown's absence, Edgecombe's increased playing time stands out and likely raises the second-year guard's fantasy ceiling in the short term.

- Raptors' injury woes continue as Barrett, CMB go down: Already playing without Kawhi Leonard, whose knee injury has kept him out since October 23, Toronto lost RJ Barrett (hamstring) and Collin Murray-Boyles (concussion protocol) during Week 5. Barrett will be re-evaluated in two weeks, while Murray-Boyles' timeline depends on how soon he's cleared to resume full activity. Those injuries have freed up opportunities for multiple players, including Jamison Battle and first-round pick Allen Graves.

In games against the Wizards and Pistons, Battle totaled 29 points (11-of-21 FGs), 10 rebounds, one steal and seven three-pointers. While he started against Washington and played 33 minutes, Battle was used in a reserve role in the November 20 win over Detroit, playing 20 minutes. As for Graves, he had a career night against Detroit, finishing with 20 points (6-of-9 FGs, 6-of-6 FTs), 10 rebounds, one assist, three steals, one block and two three-pointers. While he came off the bench for his lone season at Santa Clara, Graves' advanced numbers were a big reason he was selected in the first round. Like Murray-Boyles, he has the tools needed to be a reliable category-league option for fantasy managers.

- Anthony Edwards (knee) reveals that he has no return timeline: Sidelined since the final week of October after undergoing an arthroscopic procedure on his right knee, Edwards met with the media ahead of the Timberwolves' November 19 loss to the Knicks. The Timberwolves star said he doesn't have a firm return timeline, so it could go longer than the initially reported two-month timeline. Ayo Dosunmu has been the replacement in the starting lineup, and he's performed well, averaging 19.7 points, 4.5 rebounds, 3.2 assists, 1.6 steals and 2.4 three-pointers in 13 starts.

Jaden McDaniels is enjoying the best individual season of his NBA career, while LaMelo Ball and Jonathan Kuminga have fit in well with his new team. Since losing Edwards, the Timberwolves have gone 9-4 and looked like a team more than capable of holding down the fort until their star returns. The one concern for Minnesota may be Rudy Gobert, who has continued to struggle offensively. His scoring average of 7.6 points per game is the lowest since his rookie season (2013-14).

- Austin Reaves (heel) should return...but when?: Reaves has missed the Lakers' last four games with a left heel contusion, but he could be back "soon." Ahead of Friday's win over the Kings, Lakers head coach JJ Redick said that he hoped to get Reaves back within the next two games. If Reaves can't play on Monday against the Jazz, the next possible return day would be the day after Thanksgiving against the Spurs in San Antonio. Quentin Grimes has performed well as the replacement in the starting lineup, hitting 10 three-pointers and dropping 47 points in an overtime win over the Hornets, but the Lakers also need Reaves back to ease some of the pressure on Luka Dončić as a playmaker.

- Stephen Curry (oblique) to be re-evaluated: Injured on November 11, Curry was given a re-evaluation timeline of two weeks, which falls in the middle of Week 6. Obviously, he isn't guaranteed to return to the court immediately, but the Warriors desperately need their star guard back. Not only has Golden State lost all four games that Curry's missed, but they also haven't been competitive. During this five-game losing streak, which began with the game against the Rockets in which Curry was injured, the Warriors have led for a total of 46 seconds and have four wire-to-wire defeats.

While Yaxel Lendeborg has done what he can in Curry's absence, making him the primary scoring option puts him in an incredibly difficult spot. Lendeborg's efficiency has suffered, while Brandin Podziemski has scored 15 points or less in each of the Warriors' last four games.

- Evan Mobley is expected to be re-evaluated: Mobley has been out since the Cavaliers' November 11 win over the Knicks with a toe injury, and on November 16 it was reported that he would be re-evaluated in 7-10 days. Given the timeline, he should be re-evaluated early in Week 6, with Cleveland playing three games next week. They'll play road games against the Bulls (Monday) and Knicks (Wednesday) before hosting the Heat on Sunday. Tyrese Proctor initially moved into the starting lineup after Peyton Watson was sidelined for the November 14 win over the Mavericks, and he's remained in that role since Watson returned.

In four starts, Proctor averaged 13.5 points, 4.0 rebounds, 3.8 assists and 2.3 three-pointers per game, shooting 47.7 percent from the field and 60 percent from the foul line. It should be noted that the first game of that stretch, the win over Dallas, was played without Cleveland's entire starting five. Proctor's production has decreased game by game, but Monday's matchup with the Bulls could be a good one for him.

- Norman Powell (hamstring) to be re-evaluated during Week 6: Powell has been diagnosed with a "mild" strain of his right hamstring and is expected to be re-evaluated during Week 6. The Bulls open their Week 6 at home against the Cavaliers on Monday, so that game can likely be ruled out. Chicago finishes the week with games against the Hornets (Wednesday) and the Pacers (Saturday), so fantasy managers may not get anything out of Powell.

With Isaac Okoro already in the starting lineup due to Caleb Wilson's broken hand, Buddy Hield could be the player who steps in for Powell. The veteran guard has scored in double figures in four of his last five games, and he's made at least two three-pointers in five straight games. Hield doesn't qualify as a must-add player, but he's worth watching in the Bulls' Week 5 finale against the Nuggets on November 22.

- Atlanta, Dallas and Washington have two-game weeks: The Hawks, Mavericks and Wizards play two games in Week 6. However, while Dallas and Washington will each play a game before Thanksgiving, both of Atlanta's games will be played after the holiday. The Hawks have been without Kingston Flemings (back) and CJ McCollum (calf) recently, with the latter not expected to be re-evaluated for another three weeks. Aaron Wiggins has moved into the starting lineup, while the playmaking responsibilities have been shared among Nickeil Alexander-Walker, Dyson Daniels and Jalen Johnson. Hopefully, the time off is enough for Flemings to return if he can't play in Atlanta's Week 5 finale against the Pistons on November 22.

1. Will Jaylen Brown be able to return during Week 6?

2. Will Tyrese Haliburton (hamstring) return before the start of December?

3. How close is Paolo Banchero (ankle) to a return, and how will it impact Franz Wagner?

For release at 10:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#2886. Rockets must choose carefully in strange 2027 NBA Draft

If the 2026-27 NBA season ended today, the Houston Rockets would have roughly a 64% chance of landing a top-five pick.

I thought it was roughly 80%. The Brooklyn Nets and Phoenix Suns both have 40%-ish odds; add them together, 40%.

I was, as is so often the case, corrected on X. If each independent event has a 40% chance of happening, each has a 60% chance of not happening. So anyway, long and short, accounting for that, it's about 64%.

Remember when Rafael Stone said not to judge the James Harden trade until 2030? Well, we're almost in 2027, and it feels pretty safe to credit Stone's vision. The Rockets are 12-4; it's nearly certain that they'll have a lottery pick (and probably two) this summer, and there's a pretty good chance one of those picks lands in the top five. Life is good.

There's always a catch, isn't there? The Rockets draft one of the best centers of all time in the same draft that the best player of all time is drafted in. They change NBA offense, but another team changes it even more. Whatever.

The catch is that this draft is widely viewed as weak. If you've researched it, you've likely come to the same conclusion. There's Kansas' Tyran Stokes, and then…yeah.

At least, the list of (nearly) surefire NBA stars begins and ends with Stokes. That doesn't mean there aren't quality players in the draft:

It means Rafael Stone will have to dig for them.

Caveat One: We're early in the draft cycle. It's entirely possible that someone whose name isn't in the most thorough Excel file on the most obsessive aspiring internet scout's laptop.

Caveat Two: I wrote a whole piece about how the Rockets should be looking at Baba Oladotun, and he's averaging a red-hot 4, 2, and 1. Have your grain of salt on hand.

Right now, I'm fixated on two particular prospects. Illinois' Quentin Coleman is a 6'4″ combo guard. I'm convinced he's got a distinctly positive wingspan – I'd estimate 6'8″ – but Google has no idea. He's averaging 17.8 points, 6.8 rebounds, and 4.4 assists while shooting 55.6% from the field and 37.0% from three-point range on 5.4 attempts per game.

The cool thing about Coleman is that he's a lead ball-handler who plays with the energy of a role player. He reminds me of a young Lonzo Ball, with maybe a bit more on-ball juice and a bit less defense.

The other is Ivan Kharchenkov, a 6'7″, 230-pound wing on Arizona. Kharchenkov almost entered the draft last year. He's an outstanding, aggressive perimeter defender with some ancillary skills: He can pass and dribble some. The problem with Kharchenkov has always been his low three-point volume, which never bodes well for an NBA role player.

This year, Kharchenkov is forcing us to ask a question: What if he isn't a role player? He's averaging 22.6 points, 8.0 rebounds, and 4.6 assists per game.

There are concerns about Kharchenkov. He's still shooting just 4.0 threes per game. It's also been suggested that his strength-based shot creation won't work in the NBA, where men are stronger. If he's forced to fend for himself, Kharchenkov tends to muscle into his defender with a few dribbles to create space for his mid-range.

Those are my guys. You may have your own. The broader point is this: Very few "bad" NBA drafts are as bad as they were perceived to be in hindsight. It does happen, but more often, there are stars hiding in plain sight. At this convergence of fortune and strategy, all the Rockets can do is choose carefully. It could be the difference between finding the would-be best team in the NBA's missing piece, and adding another dud to rot away in the 8th man spot until he's trade fodder.

For now, let's wait and see what the Draft Gods decide.

#2884. 5 momentous plays in Week 11 include Ravens' mistakes, DK Metcalf's ejection and J.J. McCarthy's timely tackle

In a "Momentous Plays" first, one team has snagged the top two spots this week. Take a bow, Baltimore Ravens.

They were hardly alone in the Hall of Ignominy on Sunday, joined by noted scofflaw DK Metcalf's latest affront.

Our final two momentous plays fall more into the "bad luck" and "great football play" categories, giving us a nice variety.

Here are the five most momentous plays from Sunday's early and afternoon games.

The Ravens coughed up Sunday's game in a colorful variety of ways. The first big shock was Justice Hill's brilliant run … which was capped by a gutting ending.

Baltimore led 12-7 and was deep in Carolina territory when Hill peeled off what appeared to be a 36-yard touchdown. But out of nowhere came Panthers cornerback Will Lee, who ripped the ball out at the final second. The fumble bounced out of the back of the end zone, giving the ball back to the Panthers in a shocking turn of events.

Replays showed just how close Hill was to the end zone when Lee saved the day. Panthers head coach Dave Canales later called the forced fumble one of his favorite plays of his tenure.

The Ravens forced a punt on the next series, but — get this — they muffed the punt, setting the Panthers up in glorious field position. A few plays later, the Panthers scored a touchdown, giving them the lead at 14-12.

It was basically a 14-point swing — but the Panthers' lead was short-lived, as the Ravens ran back the ensuing kickoff for a touchdown, putting them back ahead.

This easily could've been a Ravens blowout had a few plays gone differently early. And they weren't done yet!

A wild game that saw seven lead changes came down to the Ravens driving late and trailing 35-33 with just over a minute left. Even after all their mistakes, they had a chance to steal a road game and keep their dim playoff hopes alive.

Jacoby Brissett, who had a big game, was sacked with 1:30 remaining. About 25 seconds then ticked off the clock before he dumped a short pass to Hill, leading the Ravens to use their first timeout with 59 seconds remaining.

Brissett converted a fourth-and-1 sneak at his 31-yard line. The next snap came with 36 seconds left. The Ravens had three timeouts left at the start of the drive, and they had let almost a minute run off the clock to run four plays.

Again, they were at least 30 yards from field-goal range. It was putrid clock management, and it seemed to make the Ravens tighter as they tried to win.

Brissett's next pass was tipped by the omnipresent Lee and intercepted by Tyrel Dodson to seal a Panthers win. The Ravens' sixth straight loss would seem to make a late-season Lamar Jackson return even less likely.

DK Metcalf's latest misstep cost the Steelers on Sunday.

Metcalf drew two personal foul calls in Sunday's 19-16 loss at the Eagles, getting disqualified with just under five minutes remaining in the third quarter. The second penalty came after a third-and-8 incompletion, and Metcalf wasn't directly involved in the play, with the ball going in the direction of Germie Bernard.

Metcalf "just missed" poking the eye of the Eagles' Cooper DeJean, the Philadelphia defensive back told reporters postgame. He and Metcalf had been jawing all game to that point. The penalty pushed the Steelers back, though the immediate effect of his ejection wasn't felt, as Chris Boswell nailed the field goal to cut the Eagles' lead to 16-9.

Still, Metcalf left the game with one 2-yard reception and was gone for the remainder of a three-point Steelers loss. If there was a bright side, his absence gave an unexpected opportunity to Max Hurleman, who made multiple impressive catches, including a TD, in his NFL debut.

But with all due respect to Hurleman, for whom we're rooting, those catches should have been to the receiver the Steelers are paying $33 million per year.

The Buccaneers trailed 10-7 at halftime in Detroit. It appeared that Tampa Bay was about to take a second-half lead on its opening drive of the third quarter.

Irving ripped off an untouched 80-yard touchdown run, but the referees had other ideas. Luke Goedeke was flagged for holding, wiping out the play, and it appeared to be the right call, as Goedeke pulled the Lions defender to the ground.

Instead of a crowd-silencing TD, it was a first-and-long situation, backed up in Tampa's own end. Baker Mayfield's third-and-17 pass a few plays later came up a yard short, and the Bucs had to punt.

The Lions pushed the lead to 17-7 about three minutes later, with Jared Goff hitting Jameson Williams on a 57-yard bomb. The Bucs eventually cut the deficit to 17-14 before losing 31-21 in a game they never led.

J.J. McCarthy finished Sunday's win over the Jaguars with 173 yards passing, 33 more rushing, one touchdown pass and one critical tackle.

The Giants missed a field-goal try to open the second half, but their defense — which played great all game — forced the Jaguars to a three-and-out. Trying to pad a 20-6 lead, the Giants drove to the Jaguars' 32-yard line when disaster struck. Cam Skattebo coughed up a first-and-10 handoff, and Jaguars safety Antonio Johnson scooped it up.

Johnson had the look of a man trying to house it, continuing to look behind him for potential stalkers with a lot of green in front of him. But McCarthy raced over toward the sideline, and Johnson didn't appear to see him until it was too late. The QB took him down as the would-be free safety on the play.

The 42-yard return appeared to be the break the Jaguars needed, but it turned out that McCarthy's tackle saved seven points. The Jaguars turned the ball over on downs, and though the Giants couldn't immediately take advantage, saving the points was critical.

The Jaguars scored on three of their final four drives, and the Giants added only one more field goal, but McCarthy's tackle and Paulson Adebo's fourth-quarter interception of Trevor Lawrence were the massive plays the Giants needed to hang on.

#2880. ANALYSIS: Does Nets’ closing problem have solution?

The idea of having the best of both worlds sounds nice, right?

Unfortunately, in life, things typically don't work that way. There are trade-offs in just about everything. The frustrating part is that either choice feels wrong, especially in the NBA. But neither choice is exactly right, either.

It's an interesting conundrum, especially if you are the 2026-27 Brooklyn Nets.

Through 15 games, Brooklyn, sitting at 3-12, has somehow managed to lose five games by 15+ points. It's the most in the league. However, the squad has also dropped four games by five points or fewer.

That's what makes the Nets' start to the season so interesting. There have certainly been several blowouts, and the squad has only three wins, but there is also a chunk of games where Brooklyn was in it until the final buzzer. That includes Friday's tough 113-111 loss to the Orlando Magic.

According to Cleaning the Glass, Brooklyn has won 1.4 fewer games than their point differential would suggest, the fifth-worst mark in the league. Based on their -7.1 point differential, the Nets would be expected to have 4.4 wins through 15 games.

When those games get tight, the Nets just haven't been able to pull away consistently. Despite having the NBA's highest effective field goal percentage in the fourth quarter, their shooting drops off significantly in clutch situations — the final few minutes of games. The Nets are shooting just 37.2% from the field in those moments, ranking 23rd in the league. Their free-throw shooting has been even worse, sitting at 60%, the fourth-worst mark.

Subsequently, the Nets' winning percentage in clutch situations is just 20.0%, the third-lowest clip in the NBA. The biggest problem comes with closing games, not necessarily being competitive or keeping it close entering the fourth.

Is it coaching? Is it execution? Is it the lack of a closer? The Nets seem to be searching for those same answers themselves.

However, from afar, Jordi Fernández looks to be facing a deeper problem that could be out of control. There is only so much he can do with his rotations. Even on Friday night, he switched up a multitude of components in his minutes distribution, including starting Danny Wolf over Egor Dëmin, which ultimately panned out.

In large part, the overarching issue with Brooklyn comes back to the trade-off element. Want more scoring? There's a good chance you're sacrificing defense. Prioritize the other end? You probably have to sacrifice scoring.

Fernández isn't entirely absolved of blame, but the Nets lack legitimate two-way players. And when you're trying to win a game in the final minutes, particularly against more talented teams, that only makes it harder.

The Nets have been stuck in a tough predicament, trying to balance what matters most in late-game situations: offense or defense. That has cost them in multiple games.

At the end of the day, the Nets don't have an established closing lineup. And that's hard to overcome in today's NBA, especially when games slow down in the final few minutes, and there's little margin for error.

Think about some of the better teams in the league, some that Brooklyn has even played. When a game gets tight, there is a pretty solid understanding of who's getting the basketball. Most solid squads have that guy they trust in crucial situations to turn to when things start to break down.

First off, who is that guy for the Nets? Michael Porter Jr. is probably the closest thing. He has been their most dependable offensive weapon, but is he really that high-end takeover guy?

That's where the roster's lack of two-way versatility becomes even more problematic. When Fernández needs offense, he has to consider which players give Brooklyn the best chance to score. However, in many cases, that comes at the expense of defense. That's what's so frustrating with the state of the roster.

Unfortunately for the Nets, it's not as simple as just putting "the five best players" out on the court. It's simply not how the roster is constructed: eight players are on rookie contracts, and the team is the second-youngest in the NBA over the past 25 years, older than only last year's group.

The Nets lack a proven guy who can dominate games down the stretch. Obviously that comes over time, and for a rebuilding team like Brooklyn, a player like that doesn't emerge overnight. The hope is that Mikel Brown Jr. can become that guy, and the early returns on the rookie are promising so far, but he is still just 12 games into his NBA career while also being on a minutes restriction.

Individually, Brooklyn has pieces that can be useful. It's been promising to see growth from players like Nolan Traoré and Danny Wolf, looking to take legitimate second-year leaps. Julius Randle and MPJ add veteran experience and scoring punch. Then there is someone like Day'Ron Sharpe who has impressed while emerging as the team's starting big.

Those players can be useful. Collectively, though, it's tough to find a five-man combination that doesn't leave something to be desired.

For a young team, there is some encouragement in being competitive. Hanging with high-level teams down the stretch like the Boston Celtics and the New York Knicks is encouraging, but when will those tight games actually translate into wins? There is also a distinction between being competitive but losing and losing games that are supposed to be wins, especially against strong competition. The latter can't always be used as an excuse.

The head coach isn't completely out of this conversation. A few of his rotational decisions, even with the newer changes, have been questionable. Look no further than Noah Clowney.

Clowney, 22, is in the final year of his rookie contract. While he's still 22, he hasn't produced much consistently. That's been the biggest concern with the Alabama product since he entered the league.

Through 15 games, though, he hasn't done much to change that narrative. In 21 minutes per game, he is averaging 6.5 points, 4.2 rebounds, and 1.1 assists on 41.3% from the field.

Furthermore, he owns a team-low -107 plus/minus. For context, that's also a 17-percentage-point difference between him and the second-lowest-rated net: Mo Wagner at -90. Meanwhile, his 3-point percentage (27.7%) is also the lowest on the team among qualified shooters (minimum 3.0 attempts per game).

There have been some glimpses. He had back-to-back games against Memphis and New Orleans where he finished with at least 15 points and two 3s. Overall, though, not much has changed. The flashes are still there, but Clowney continues to struggle to find that level of consistency.

And that brings us back to Friday night, when he finished with zero points in 22 minutes, shooting 0-of-4 from the field, and recording a team-worst minus-12 rating. Yet, with the game on the line, he was still on the floor, defending Franz Wagner, who hit the game-winning shot over him.

Now Wagner had a tremendous game, finishing with 35 points, and his offensive ability is highly touted. However, putting Clowney out there in that situation was questionable to begin with.

If he isn't providing offensively, what exactly is the team gaining by keeping him out there? While he came away with a block and a steal, he hasn't been regarded as a plus defender, especially for his position and size, at 6'10" and 210 pounds. Most glaringly, his plus/minus rating was not only the lowest on Friday, but it's also the lowest among the whole team this year, and by a pretty wide margin.

Despite that, though, he continues to earn consistent minutes. He ranks eighth on the team in minutes per game (21.0), behind Randle (33.1), Porter (32.9), Dëmin (24.0), Sharpe (23.9), Wolf (23.0), Brown (22.9), and Traoré (22.3). So esentially, he's been the Nets' third player off the bench this season. That's a pretty important role.

Clearly, with him in that role and out on the court to close games, things haven't been working too well. That's where Fernández comes in. He needs to be willing to make more on-the-fly adjustments to the rotation, especially when things aren't working.

How about looking at players like Josh Minott and Drake Powell? Neither has received notable minutes this season out of garbage time. Neither is a proven NBA defender, but is it time to give them minutes to see what happens? It's worked with Danny Wolf.

After all, isn't that supposed to be part of a rebuilding season? Minott, 23, shot 39.5% from deep wth Brooklyn last year after being acquired at the trade deadline, and the organization saw enough to award him a two-year, $9 million extension in the offseason. At 6'8" and 205 pounds, he also has the tools to be a disruptor on the defensive end.

Powell, meanwhile, has spent much of the season in the G League so far with Brooklyn's affiliate, the Long Island Nets. He has played in five NBA games and was called up for Friday, although most of his minutes have come in garbage time. The North Carolina product boasts a 43-inch vertical and a 7-foot wingspan, giving him intriguing potential on offense and defense, notably at the point of attack. That was on display at points last year. In fact, his 91.9 defensive rating led the NBA last December, and the Nets actually went 7-4 that month.

Of course, the Nets have a free agency decision on Clowney coming up in the Summer. And of course, the Nets are bringing Powell along slowly. He was, after all, the biggest reach of the Flatbush 5. But, of course, the offense remains a work in progress. He shot just 28% from 3 during his rookie campaign and a painful 4-of-16 from that range in Summer League, including a 0-of-14 start.

There is also two-way forward Chaney Johnson, who played a firm role in the rotation at the start of the season, but his minutes have gradually disappeared. Johnson still lacks an established offensive game, but he can guard multiple positions on both ends of the floor.

Maybe Minott struggles. Maybe Powell shows that he isn't ready. Maybe Johnson doesn't improve much either. However, getting that understanding is important, especially when things aren't working.

And if one of them does emerge as a useful piece, particularly defensively, that would be huge for the roster's current outlook.

There may not be a perfect solution. At 3-12, though, the team needs to keep experimenting, especially off the bench. Fernández' decision regarding Dëmin and Wolf on Friday paid off; now it's important to see that same mindset carry over to bench minutes.

Given the organization's overall situation, there's much more to gain from trying something different than sticking with what hasn't worked.

NFL markets measure different outcomes, from the winner to an individual player's statistics. Confirm the market and scoring period before comparing prices. These examples use hypothetical lines.

Moneylines back a team to win outright. At -150, a winning $15 cash wager earns $10 profit and returns the $15 stake.

Point spreads apply a scoring handicap. A team at -3.5 must win by four or more points. A three-point win at -3 generally produces a push, returning the stake.

Game totals concern the teams' combined points. Over 44.5 requires at least 45. Check whether the market covers the full game, a half or a quarter.

Team totals concern one team's points. A team total over 23.5 requires that team to score at least 24, regardless of its opponent's score.

Player props concern individual statistics, such as passing yards or receptions. Over 250.5 passing yards requires at least 251. Participation and injury rules affect settlement.

Futures concern outcomes decided later, such as a division winner or Super Bowl champion. Read which games count toward season win totals.

Same-game parlays combine selections from one matchup. Every active leg must win. The operator's rules determine how a voided selection affects the remaining ticket and odds.

Check the cashier's withdrawal options before choosing a deposit method. Review the minimum transfer amount, possible fees and payment-account verification. A method accepted for deposits may have different withdrawal support.

Track a withdrawal through sportsbook approval and payment delivery. An approved request may still need time to reach your bank or wallet. Check its status and reference number before contacting support about a delay.

Compare the published estimate with the amount you plan to withdraw. Confirm whether either the sportsbook or payment provider charges a fee. Check whether a verification request needs your attention.

Review cash and promotional balances separately. Bonus bets and Betr Bucks follow their own wagering and settlement rules. Use the withdrawable balance shown in the cashier when deciding how much to request.

Check eligibility. Confirm that the sportsbook and its featured offer are available where you plan to wager. Check the age requirement for both.

Open the signup link. Review the offer shown on the registration page. Enter the required code at the step specified in the terms.

Verify your account. Complete identity and location checks. Confirm which deposit methods satisfy the offer before funding the account.

Activate the reward. Opt in if required. Check the wager amount, eligible markets and any minimum odds. Apply the correct token in the bet slip when the offer requires one.

Confirm acceptance and follow-up steps. Check bet history for the accepted ticket. Use the promotions page to track later claims or qualifying wagers. For Betr Picks, follow the fantasy entry and token instructions.

Choose an outcome, such as a team winning, a game going over its total or a player reaching a yardage threshold. The bet slip shows the line, odds and potential return. The sportsbook settles the ticket under the rules for that market.

The minimum age depends on the jurisdiction and product. Many sportsbooks require customers to be 21 or older. Some markets allow sports betting at 18. Check both the sportsbook's age requirement and the terms of the promotion you plan to claim.

Do I have to pay taxes on sports betting winnings?

Yes. The IRS requires you to report gambling winnings even if you receive no Form W-2G. Keep records of your wagers and payouts for tax reporting.

What happens to my NFL player prop if a player gets injured?

Check the market's participation and injury rules. At DraftKings, Early Exit Protected can void eligible full-game props when a player leaves injured in the first half and never returns. In an eligible parlay, DraftKings removes the voided leg and recalculates the odds on the remaining selections.

Verify the operator on your state regulator's licensed sportsbook list and download the official app. Check how to set deposit limits, take a break or self-exclude before funding an account. Wagers can lose even when the operator is licensed.

Decide how much you can afford to spend before opening the bet slip. Treat that amount as a limit and avoid increasing it to qualify for a promotion. If gambling is causing problems, support is available through the National Council on Problem Gambling.

#2873. Game Preview: Knicks vs Pacers, November 22, 2026

Over the last few years, the Pacers have given the Knicks fits.

We all know about the recent postseason history between these two teams, although we won't go into that. Even with Tyrese Haliburton out last season, they battled and made the Knicks work. Outside of the March 17th game in which the Knicks won handily 136-110, the other three games were all close games. Despite being in the middle of a retooling and tanking season, they tried their hardest to beat the Knicks, and even succeeded in doing so once, handing them a 137-134 overtime loss in early February. It also isn't that long ago that the Knicks suffered what is probably the most frustrating loss of their season. Less than 24 hours after the Knicks' most exhilarating win of the season though, they get a chance to get some sweet revenge on an opponent is starting to become way too familiar.

The Pacers enter Sunday's contest 5-11 and are losers of three straight. Haliburton remains sidelined with a hamstring injury, T.J. McConnell is out also with a hamstring injury, and Pascal Siakam is currently listed as a questionable with a hip injury. But given their recent tendency to bring out their big guns for the Knicks, I'd expect Siakam to play. And given their recent ability to test the Knicks even without their starting point guard, I'd expect the Pacers to push the Knicks.

That is unfortunately even more the case considering the situation the Knicks find themselves in. They just played in a game that came down to the last shot and saw Jalen Brunson, Josh Hart, and Landry Shamet all play 35 or more minutes. They are also at risk of being without both Karl-Anthony Towns and OG Anunoby for a second straight game due to illness and calf tightness, respectively. We already saw Brunson stretched thin last night against a tough Trail Blazers defense. Having to go without the team's second and third-best offensive players against a Pacers team that tends to defend him tough on the second night of a back-to-back sounds like a pretty tough and unenviable task.

That being said, the Knicks should win this one. I know that was the case against the Pacers the last time they met up, but the Knicks are the better team. Being without Towns and Anunoby hurts them a tremendous amount on both sides of the ball. And that was evident in the final three quarters of last night's game, where we saw the Knicks' offense slowly revert to a heliocentric Brunson iso-fest. The defense held up, partly due to the Trail Blazers offense shooting themselves in the foot, but having Anunoby there obviously helps. And if Siakam is a go tonight, the rosters on paper match up more closely because I don't see who on this roster has the size to really slow him down if Anunoby is out again.

If Bridges continues playing the way he has as of late and Shamet continues to look like prime Stephen Curry reincarnated, it might not matter, though. Brunson will get a lot of shots, and he may not be the most efficient against what should be a very physical Pacers team that has gotten to know Brunson and his endless counter moves pretty well. But he's also been shooting the ball well, especially from three. And he's made everybody quickly forget that slow shooting start he's gotten off to.

When these two teams last met up on November 13th, Jordan Clarkson, Miles McBride, and the aforementioned Shamet combined to go just 5-16 for 14 points while Aaron Nesmith went for 18 points on 7-10 shooting by himself and the supporting cast of Obi Toppin, Jay Huff, Larry Nance, and Quenton Jackson went 9-17 for 22 points. Nesmith has struggled mightily from three over the last few games, in large part due to a hand sprain, hamstring injury, and knee soreness that have reportedly been bothering him. He's currently shooting a career-worst 25% on catch-and-shoot threes, is shooting just 27.4% from three overall this season, and just 22.7% from three in his last 10 games. Nesmith is a pivotal part of the Pacers team, but he's been hurting their offense recently by not even looking to shoot-something the Knicks' very own Hart has done at times as well.

With Brunson and Shamet likely getting a lot of attention and the Pacers needing as much offense as they can get with Haliburton out and Siakam's availability being in question, the game could come down to which group of role players can do more. Can Kelly Oubre, who went for 16 points when these two teams last met, repeat his performance? How about Bridges, who not only hit the game-winning shot last night but scored 18 points against the Pacers not too long ago? And how about the matchup between two glue guys in Hart and Nesmith, both of whom have struggled to score the ball effectively recently?

I expect the Knicks to win this game. But I said that for their last meeting as well. With both sides missing key players, it'll likely be a slugfest. Don't expect an aesthetically pleasing, or even an entertaining game. The Pacers aren't afraid to play that way, and as we've seen, neither are the Knicks. Not that it's necessarily mattered in the past, but the Knicks are trending in the right direction and the Pacers aren't. New York takes this 103-95.

Who: Indiana Pacers (5-11) at New York Knicks (10-6)Date: Sunday, November 22, 2026Time: 8:00 PM ETPlace: Madison Square Garden, New York, NYTV: MSG, NBA TVFollow: @ptknicksblog and bsky

#2867. What we learned as Brandin Podziemski, Warriors outlast Celtics to end skid

What we learned as Brandin Podziemski, Warriors outlast Celtics to end skid originally appeared on NBC Sports Bay Area

SAN FRANCISCO – Home is where the heart is, and it's miraculously where the Warriors snapped their five-game losing streak.

Was it pretty? No. Was it gritty? Yes. The Warriors failed to score 100 points for the fifth straight game, but survived the Boston Celtics for an 88-87 win Sunday night at Chase Center.

The most important Warriors stat of the night was offensive rebounding. Golden State secured 19 offensive rebounds for 22 second-chance points. That helped mitigate the Warriors' 20 turnovers for 24 Celtics points.

Brandin Podziemski scored five clutch points in the fourth quarter to score a team-high 18 points as the Warriors had four players reach double figures.

Celtics star Jayson Tatum scored 23 points in the first half, but the Warriors held him to only four in the second half.

Here are three takeaways from the Warriors improving to 9-8 on the season.

Threads is Green's preferred social media outlet, but on Sunday, the 36-year-old sent a plethora of posts on X (formerly known as Twitter) leading up to the Warriors' game. Most were in response to Eddie Johnson calling him out while replying to a Steve Kerr interview on 95.7 The Game where the Warriors coach said Steph Curry's absence "really limits Draymond's impact offensively."

If you thought Green would come to Chase Center fired up and determined to quiet the noise, you thought right. The Warriors began the game setting up Green for a wide-open three from the right corner. Their first six points were two Green threes, both of which he took from the right corner.

Green entered Sunday having made four threes all season. He left the first quarter with three, and a season-high nine points for an entire game.

Although Green didn't score another point the rest of the game, his defense on Tatum in the final seconds secured the Warriors' win.

Gui Santos might have been the best story in the NBA last season, earning a three-year, $15 million contract. But his encore hasn't been the same.

Struggling to find his rhythm while playing through injuries, Santos coming into Sunday night was averaging just 6.3 points per game while shooting 38.2 percent from the field and 24 percent from three. A different Santos showed up against the Celtics. Santos scored 13 points off the bench to tie his season high.

The last time he reached 13 points was nearly one month ago, on Oct. 26.

Santos also was a game-high plus-12 as an energizer who had four offensive rebounds. His signature footwork around the rim was on point, and Santos went 3 of 5 from deep for his first game of the season with multiple threes.

Although Kerr wouldn't commit to anything long term, he made it clear in his pregame press conference that he wanted to give Charles Bassey an extended look. And if all goes well, Bassey will remain the Warriors' starting center the rest of the regular season, barring roster changes.

The journeyman proved why he's plenty capable against the Celtics. By halftime, Bassey's impact was felt all over the floor, as he piled up seven points on a perfect 3-of-3 shooting, along with five rebounds, three assists and three steals.

Bassey, at 6-foot-10, allows Green to slide down to play power forward. His athleticism and scoring touch also gives the Warriors another element they're missing. Bassey has good hands around the rim and can get up for lobs, and his floater makes him a real scoring threat. It helps that he's willing to do the dirty work, too.

The five offensive rebounds Bassey grabbed says everything about the energy he brings to the Warriors. Defensively, he blew up sets and tied a career high in steals. Offensively, Bassey kept possessions alive and had his seventh game in double figures this season, ending the night with 13 points on 6-of-7 shooting.

#2865. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

#2863. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

#2860. Retirees face a looming deadline for required withdrawals — missing it could trigger a tax penalty

Every year, millions of Americans must take required withdrawals from retirement accounts by Dec. 31.

Amid the busy holiday season, it's easy to miss the year-end deadline for required minimum distributions — a mistake that can trigger a hefty IRS penalty, experts said.

"One of the most common RMD mistakes is waiting until the last minute," said certified financial planner Alex Canellopoulos, director of wealth at Vista Capital Partners in Portland, Oregon.

Most retirees must start RMDs from pretax accounts at age 73. The withdrawal is based on the previous year's account balances on Dec. 31, your age and an IRS "life expectancy factor."

The first annual RMD deadline is April 1 of the year after you turn 73, and future withdrawals are due by Dec. 31. If you wait until April 1 for the first one, you'll need two RMDs that year.

RMDs also apply to certain inherited IRAs if the original IRA owner reached RMD age before their death.

In 2023, more than 14.8 million taxpayers age 70 or older had a traditional individual retirement account, according to the latest IRS data. The average year-end market value of the accounts was about $330,000.

Deferring RMDs until the last minute raises the likelihood of mistakes, such as overlooking certain retirement accounts or making withdrawal calculation errors, Canellopoulos said.

Some 7% of Vanguard investors at RMD age missed their yearly withdrawal in 2025, up slightly from 6.7% in 2024, according to a Vanguard analysis of about 400,000 RMD-age retirees with IRA balances published in December 2025.

Among those investors, the average missed RMD was $10,500 in 2025, Vanguard told CNBC.

Investors who don't take RMDs by the annual deadline may owe an excise tax to the IRS, worth up to 25% of the amount they should have withdrawn. For example, the excise tax could be up to $2,625 for a $10,500 missed RMD.

If investors miss a required withdrawal, they could reduce the penalty to 10% if they "timely corrected" the RMD within two years and file Form 5329, according to the IRS.

In some cases, the IRS could waive the penalty entirely due to "reasonable error" if investors take "reasonable steps" to fix the shortfall, according to the agency. Investors can file Form 5329 with a letter of explanation to request the waiver.

"If you don't ask, you're not going to get it," said certified public accountant Tim Steffen, director of advanced planning at financial services firm Baird. "I've never heard of anybody being denied the waiver."

Investors who give to charity may be skipping a specific donation strategy that can reduce or eliminate their annual RMDs, according to Lawrence Pon, a CFP and CPA based in Redwood City, California.

They can use a so-called qualified charitable distribution, or QCD, Pon said.

A QCD has a dual tax benefit: It doesn't count as taxable income and may also satisfy at least part of an investor's yearly RMD, Pon said.

The move requires a direct transfer from an investor's pretax IRA to an eligible nonprofit organization. They can donate up to $111,000 in 2026. While QCD eligibility begins at age 70½, transfers won't start counting toward RMDs until an investor reaches their RMD age.

Generally, taxpayers only get a tax break for large charitable donations if they itemize deductions on their tax returns. But QCDs still provide a tax benefit for charitable gifts when investors use the standard deduction.

QCDs "provide a real benefit if you are not itemizing [tax deductions]," said Pon.

While there's no tax deduction for QCDs, the transfer won't increase a taxpayer's adjusted gross income, or AGI. Raising AGI can cause other tax consequences, such as higher premiums for Medicare Part B and Part D.

#2857. Buccaneers Week 11 Top Performers at Detroit Lions

After a week of prep time to tweak an anemic passing offense and get healthy for a second-half desperation push, the Buccaneers still suffered from familiar issues in a 31-21 loss to the Detroit Lions.

Dropping to 3-7, the Bucs face a déjà vu moment next week facing a must-win against the division rival (and leading) Carolina Panthers on Monday Night Football. The last time this season it was truly "must-win," they pulverized Carolina to the tune of 43-13 in Week 7. It's probably not reasonable to expect a similar result, but it's certainly a winnable game and the opener of the team's easiest stretch of the year.

Ultimately, it's still a deeply flawed team that's going to live and die week-to-week on the defense being the best version of itself and the run game driving the offense. Hard way to consistently win in the modern NFL.

We'll give some shoutouts and move onto the next one.

Even on a down day, Irving still served as the primary driver for the Bucs' offense. He finished with 21 touches for 100 total yards and a touchdown, but it should've been much more if a needless holding penalty from Luke Goedeke didn't erase an excellent 80-yard home run. Nevertheless, Irving continued his Pro Bowl worthy campaign, and he'll need to mirror his dominance against the Panthers from last month (106 yards, 2 TDs) to give the Bucs a chance next week.

Kenny Gainwell continued to be useful in his change-of-pace role, totaling 8 touches for 59 yards. Against a tough run defense in Detroit, both of the team's top options delivered.

Much fewer positive notes for the passing game (unsurprisingly).

Baker Mayfield is a complete liability. You can blame the injuries or the coordinator or the receivers, and they all share some blame, but ultimately your $165 million QB shouldn't be terrified at any remote sign of pressure and be able to execute some basic throws under duress.

Mayfield's pressure-to-sack ratio, which attempts to demonstrate how a quarterback's sack percentage relates to an offensive line's pressure allowed rate, was an egregious 36% Sunday. Out of 44 dropbacks, he was pressured only 11 times yet sacked 4 times. Too often the eyes are dropping when the pocket muddies and he attempts to turn into a fullback, and it simply isn't winning football.

He got the ball to rookie Ted Hurst quite a bit, and that was nice to see since Hurst has struggled mightily with drops. He reeled in 5 catches for 56 yards and 2 touchdowns, and hopefully this performance will do well to build the third-rounder's confidence and take a stronger role in the receiver room.

Not many highlights to speak of on defense, which held tough for three quarters but ultimately couldn't remain sturdy against one of the league's best offenses.

Most notably, David Walker continued to be the team's most reliable pass rusher. Toting one of the highest pressure rates in the entire league despite never starting, Walker recorded his team-leading 5th sack, the only one of the afternoon, when running the hoop on rookie tackle Blake Miller to take down Jared Goff. Perhaps the next coaching staff will let him be a starter next year.

Losing Jacob Parrish early to a back injury crippled the secondary, but Zyon McCollum continued to play very well and kept top target Amon-Ra St. Brown quiet most of the afternoon — the latter netted just 5 catches for 37 yards and a touchdown, and his score came on the opposite side of the field with Benjamin Morrison in coverage. Speaking of whom, Morrison got absolutely dogwalked all afternoon long and then left with what must be his 5th 0r 6th hamstring issue since arriving in Tampa last year. If he and Parrish are both out next week, the Bucs could be facing a very dire situation at cornerback.

Josiah Trotter continued to be a tackle machine, recording 14 total tackles on the afternoon. The coverage reps can be shaky, but he's not a liability at least and that's encouraging to see. A phantom facemask call put a damper on things at the end of the game, but such is life with NFL officiating sometimes.

McLaughlin remained perfect on his kicks for the year, the only kicker in the league to do so. He attempted no field goals but made all three of his extra points, so hurray!

McCollum made a nice play on Detroit's first field goal attempt, jumping over the line to seemingly affect Lions kicker Jake Bates on a missed attempt. After his other excellent special teams play against the Bears, McCollum is making an impact everywhere.

#2855. Kings reportedly signing veteran Gabe Vincent to be backup point guard

This had been rumored for weeks. Veteran point guard Gabe Vincent had worked out for the Sacramento Kings and had talked with them about coming in as their backup to rookie Darius Acuff Jr., he reportedly even had a standing offer.

Now, it's finally happening, a story broken by Shams Charania of ESPN and confirmed by other reports.

Vincent, 30, is signing for a veteran minimum contract, and the Kings had an open roster spot, so no other move is needed. Vincent was invited to Knicks training camp to compete for a roster spot, and reportedly also had conversations with Atlanta before the season, but chose not to join either team.

This is a full-circle moment for Vincent, who was born in Modesto (about 75 miles from Sacramento) and played his high school ball in Stockton, about 45 miles away from the Golden One Center where the Kings play. He went on to play his college ball at UC Santa Barbara.

The Kings have been seven points per 100 possessions worse this season with Acuff off the court, they needed a veteran backup point guard after losing Ben Simmons to an Achilles tear before the season. Vincent is being brought in both to stabilize the second unit and to mentor Acuff. Vincent, a seven-year NBA veteran, fills that role. Vincent isn't an elite scorer or shooter (he has averaged 6.7 points per game over his career and is shooting under 40%), but he understands how to play off talented teammates and could work well with Kings such as Domantas Sabonis and Zach LaVine.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released results from a survey of senior financial officers at banks about their strategies and practices for managing reserve balances. The Senior Financial Officer Survey is used by the Board to obtain information about banks' reserve balance management strategies and practices, their expectations around the size of their balance sheets and deposit balances their activity in money markets, and their views on other matters, such as operations on weekends and holidays.

The survey was conducted in collaboration with the Federal Reserve Bank of New York between September 18, 2026, and September 28, 2026, and includes responses from banks that held around three-fourths of total banking system reserve balances at the time of the survey.
